<h4>Background</h4>Trifluridine-tipiracil (FTD-TPI) plus bevacizumab is the standard treatment for refractory metastatic colorectal cancer. However, the conventional schedule is frequently associated with haematological and gastrointestinal toxicity, which is clinically significant and relevant to patients. A simplified 14-day schedule has been proposed to reduce toxicity, but whether this treatment preserves survival has not been established in a randomised trial. We therefore aimed to assess whether the 14-day schedule is non-inferior to the conventional schedule for overall survival.<h4>Methods</h4>PRABITAS was a pragmatic, multicentre, open-label, randomised, phase 3, non-inferiority trial conducted in 178 hospitals in Japan. Patients with refractory metastatic colorectal cancer were randomly assigned (1:1) using a minimisation algorithm, stratified by Eastern Cooperative Oncology Group performance status, RAS mutational status, and institution, to FTD-TPI (35 mg/m<sup>2</sup> orally twice daily) plus bevacizumab (5 mg/kg intravenously) on either a conventional 28-day (FTD-TPI on days 1-5 and 8-12; bevacizumab on days 1 and 15) or 14-day schedule (FTD-TPI on days 1-5; bevacizumab on day 1). Eligibility criteria were broadened compared with those of conventional explanatory trials, and trial procedures were aligned with routine clinical practice. The primary endpoint was overall survival, analysed in the intention-to-treat population, with a prespecified non-inferiority margin of 1·25. Safety was assessed in all patients who received at least one dose. Missing data were not imputed. This trial is registered with the Japan Registry of Clinical Trials, jRCTs041230120, and is closed to enrolment.<h4>Findings</h4>Between Dec 26, 2023, and May 20, 2025, we randomly assigned 972 patients, of whom 970 (99%) constituted the intention-to-treat population after two were excluded because they were randomly assigned before site approval (486 [50%] to the 28-day treatment group, 292 [60%] men and 194 [40%] women]; and 484 [50%] to the 14-day treatment group, 264 [55%] men and 220 [45%] women). Median follow-up was 17·9 months (IQR 14·3-22·6). Median overall survival was 10·3 months (95% CI 9·5-11·6) in the 28-day group and 11·3 months (10·2-12·5) in the 14-day group (stratified hazard ratio 0·93 [95% CI 0·80-1·08]). The 12-month overall survival rates were 44·0% (95% CI 39·5-48·5) in the 28-day group and 47·5% (42·9-52·0) in the 14-day group. Neutropenia grade 3 or worse was reduced in the 14-day group (13% vs 39%; absolute difference 25 percentage points [95% CI 20-31]), as was anorexia grade 2 or worse (15% vs 26%; absolute difference 11 percentage points [6-16]). Two treatment-related deaths occurred in the 14-day group and none in the 28-day group.<h4>Interpretation</h4>A 14-day schedule of FTD-TPI plus bevacizumab was non-inferior to the conventional 28-day schedule for overall survival, while reducing haematological and gastrointestinal toxicity. These findings support this simplified schedule as the new standard for FTD-TPI plus bevacizumab in refractory metastatic colorectal cancer.<h4>Funding</h4>Japan Society for the Promotion of Science KAKENHI Grant-in-Aid for Challenging Research (Pioneering).

The efficacy and safety of anbenitamab, a HER2-targeted bispecific antibody, plus albumin-bound docetaxel (HB1801) in this context are unknown.<h4>Methods</h4>In this phase 3, multicenter, open-label, randomized trial conducted in China, we randomly assigned patients with HER2-positive advanced breast cancer in a 1:1 ratio to receive either anbenitamab plus HB1801 or THP. The primary end point was progression-free survival, as assessed by blinded independent central review. Objective response was a secondary end point.<h4>Results</h4>A total of 885 patients were enrolled (445 were assigned to receive anbenitamab plus HB1801 and 440 to receive THP). At this prespecified interim analysis (with 401 events [disease progression or death] and a median follow-up of 21.0 months), the estimated 24-month progression-free survival was significantly higher with anbenitamab plus HB1801 than with THP (58.5% vs. 34.4%; hazard ratio for progression or death, 0.46; 95% confidence interval, 0.37 to 0.56; P<0.001). The percentage of patients with an objective response was 87.6% in the anbenitamab group and 73.4% in the THP group. Adverse events of grade 3 or higher occurred in 204 of 444 patients (45.9%) in the anbenitamab group and in 151 of 439 patients (34.4%) in the THP group. Neutropenia was the most common adverse event of grade 3 or higher in both groups (13.1% in the anbenitamab group and 8.4% in the THP group). Adverse events leading to death occurred in 2 patients in each group.<h4>Conclusions</h4>Anbenitamab plus HB1801 led to significantly higher progression-free survival than THP among patients with HER2-positive advanced breast cancer. (Funded by Shanghai JMT-Bio Technology and others; HEALER ClinicalTrials.gov number, NCT05838066.).

<h4>Background</h4>The standard of care for patients with muscle-invasive bladder cancer continues to evolve. The key secondary end point was overall survival.<h4>Results</h4>In total, 233 patients were assigned to the durvalumab-enfortumab vedotin group, 233 to the durvalumab-tremelimumab-enfortumab vedotin group, and 229 to the control group. Perioperative durvalumab plus enfortumab vedotin led to significantly better overall survival outcomes than surgery alone. Grade 3 or higher adverse events of any cause were noted in more than half the patients in the durvalumab groups. (Funded by AstraZeneca; VOLGA ClinicalTrials.gov number, NCT04960709; EudraCT number, 2020-005452-38.).

<h4>Importance</h4>In randomized clinical trials, sodium-glucose cotransporter 2 (SGLT2) inhibitor use was associated with increased hemoglobin and hematocrit compared with placebo. However, the magnitude and importance of this association remain unclear.<h4>Objective</h4>To evaluate the associations between SGLT2 inhibitor use and levels of hemoglobin, hematocrit, and markers of iron metabolism and erythropoiesis compared with placebo or active comparator.<h4>Data sources</h4>PubMed, Web of Science, and the Cochrane Central Register of Controlled Trials (CENTRAL) were searched from inception to July 22, 2026.<h4>Study selection</h4>Randomized, double-masked clinical trials comparing SGLT2 inhibitor use with placebo or active comparators in patients with 1 or more of the following conditions: cardiovascular disease, diabetes, heart failure, and chronic kidney disease.<h4>Data extraction and synthesis</h4>Three reviewers independently extracted data and results were pooled using random-effects meta-analysis.<h4>Main outcomes and measures</h4>Primary outcomes were changes in hemoglobin and hematocrit. Secondary outcomes were changes in red blood cell concentration, erythropoietin (EPO), and iron measures (serum iron, ferritin, transferrin saturation, and hepcidin).<h4>Results</h4>A total of 85 randomized clinical trials were analyzed (median [IQR] duration, 24 [12-32] weeks), including 70 543 participants (mean [SD] age, 62.4 [9.4] years; 25 334 female [35.9%]). Mean (SD) baseline hemoglobin was 13.6 (1.5) g/dL; baseline hematocrit was 41.5% (4.9). Seventy-eight clinical trials (67 208 participants) compared SGLT2 inhibitors with placebo, and 7 clinical trials (3335 participants) used active comparators, most commonly sitagliptin (2 trials [1158 participants]). From baseline to follow-up, hemoglobin changed by 0.55 g/dL (95% CI, 0.49 to 0.61 g/dL; P < .001) in the SGLT2 inhibitor group, and -0.08 g/dL (95% CI, -0.13 to -0.03 g/dL; P = .003) in the control group (difference, 0.60 g/dL; 95% CI, 0.55 to 0.66 g/dL; P < .001). From baseline to follow-up, hematocrit changed by 2.17% (95% CI, 1.95% to 2.39%; P < .001) in the SGLT2 inhibitor group and -0.13% (95% CI, -0.28% to 0.01%; P = .07) in the control group (difference, 2.36%; 95% CI, 2.19% to 2.52%; P < .001). Of the 6 secondary outcomes, 4 showed significant associations with SGLT2 inhibitors, including increased EPO (2.35 mIU/mL in SGLT2 inhibitor group vs 0.04 mIU/mL in control group; difference, 2.11 mIU/mL [95% CI, 0.5-3.71 mIU/mL]; P = .01) and decreased transferrin saturation (-2.2% in SGLT2 inhibitor group vs -0.10% in control group; difference, -0.84% [95% CI, -1.21% to -0.47%]; P < .001).<h4>Conclusions and relevance</h4>In a meta-analysis of randomized clinical trials, compared with control, SGLT2 inhibitor use was associated with small statistically significant increases in hemoglobin and hematocrit levels. The clinical significance of these findings is unclear.

Results of the prespecified interim analysis (data-cutoff date, July 7, 2026) are reported here.<h4>Results</h4>A total of 563 patients were randomly assigned to receive tarlatamab-durvalumab (282 patients) or durvalumab (281 patients). At a median follow-up of 15.9 months in the tarlatamab-durvalumab group and 16.1 months in the durvalumab group, the estimated median overall survival was 21.1 months (95% confidence interval [CI], 20.8 to could not be estimated) with tarlatamab-durvalumab and 12.7 months (95% CI, 11.2 to 14.8) with durvalumab (hazard ratio for death, 0.42; 95% CI, 0.32 to 0.54; P<0.001). The median progression-free survival was 7.4 months (95% CI, 5.6 to 9.1) with tarlatamab-durvalumab and 1.9 months (95% CI, 1.9 to 2.0) with durvalumab (hazard ratio for disease progression or death, 0.34; 95% CI, 0.28 to 0.42; P<0.001). Cytokine release syndrome, mainly of grade 1 or 2, was seen in nearly half the patients receiving tarlatamab-durvalumab.<h4>Conclusions</h4>Tarlatamab-durvalumab maintenance therapy resulted in significantly longer overall survival and progression-free survival than durvalumab alone among patients with extensive-stage small-cell lung cancer. ### [Advances in the management of renal cell carcinoma.](https://doi.org/10.1136/bmj-2026-100785)

- Source: `bmj_abstracts` · published `2026-10-26` · freshness `stale`
- Reading value: `pending` · status: `ineligible`
- Event: `seen` · identity: `pmid:42933810`

Renal cell carcinoma (RCC) is the most common form of kidney cancer, with 435 000 patients newly diagnosed worldwide each year. In addition to morphology and histology, molecular subtyping has advanced the diagnostic classification of RCC. Contemporary diagnosis relies on cross sectional imaging in the form of computed tomography or magnetic resonance imaging and selective biopsy. For localised disease, nephrectomy remains the standard of care, with partial nephrectomy being an option for smaller tumours. Ablation or stereotactic radiation is appropriate for selected patients. Adjuvant pembrolizumab provides improved disease-free and overall survival in high risk clear cell RCC. In metastatic disease, the introduction of immune checkpoint inhibitor based combinations has extended overall survival, providing a durable response in some patients. Despite these advances, primary and secondary resistance, along with the lack of predictive and prognostic biomarkers, remain a challenge.

Randomisation was stratified by disease status, histology, and previous receipt of (neo)adjuvant chemotherapy. Primary endpoints were progression-free survival per RECIST version 1.1, by blinded independent central review, and overall survival. This trial is registered at ClinicalTrials.gov, NCT05173987, and is ongoing.<h4>Findings</h4>Between Feb 22, 2022, and Dec 12, 2023, 299 participants were assigned to pembrolizumab (n=149) or carboplatin plus paclitaxel (n=150). At the cutoff for the prespecified interim analysis in the intention-to-treat population (April 24, 2026), median progression-free survival was 38·2 months (95% CI 17·9 months-not reached) in the pembrolizumab group versus 8·4 months (6·8-10·4) in the carboplatin plus paclitaxel group (hazard ratio [HR] 0·57, 95% CI 0·42-0·79; p=0·0002). Estimated progression-free survival was 75% (95% CI 67-81) versus 76% (68-82) at 6 months, 67% (59-74) versus 40% (32-48) at 12 months, and 53% (44-61) versus 30% (22-38) at 36 months. The prespecified statistical significance criterion for overall survival was not met at the interim analysis (medians not reached; HR 0·77, 95% CI 0·52-1·15; p=0·10). Grade 3 or higher treatment-related adverse events occurred in 27 (18%) of 149 participants in the pembrolizumab group and 73 (51%) of 144 in the carboplatin plus paclitaxel group. Treatment-related adverse events of any grade that occurred in at least 25% of participants in either group included alopecia (three [2%] of 149 in the pembrolizumab group, 72 [50%] of 144 in the carboplatin plus paclitaxel group), anaemia (seven [5%], 71 [49%]), nausea (seven [5%], 41 [28%]), decreased neutrophil count (five [3%], 40 [28%]), peripheral neuropathy (none, 38 [26%]), and fatigue (20 [13%], 37 [26%]). Treatment-related adverse events led to treatment discontinuation in 14 (9%) of 149 participants in the pembrolizumab group and 21 (15%) of 144 participants in the carboplatin plus paclitaxel group; they resulted in death in two participants in the pembrolizumab group (hepatic failure and bacterial meningitis [n=1 each]) and one participant in the carboplatin plus paclitaxel group (pulmonary embolism).<h4>Interpretation</h4>First-line pembrolizumab showed a statistically significant and clinically meaningful improvement in progression-free survival, with lower frequency of overall and grade 3 or above treatment-related adverse events. The study did not compare pembrolizumab monotherapy with the current standard of care (immunotherapy plus chemotherapy).<h4>Funding</h4>Merck Sharp & Dohme, a subsidiary of Merck & Co.

<h4>Background</h4>Patients with heavily pretreated hormone receptor-positive and human epidermal growth factor receptor 2-negative (HR+/HER2-) breast cancer have few treatment options. Izalontamab brengitecan (iza-bren) is a first-in-class, epidermal growth factor receptor and HER3 bispecific antibody-drug conjugate that contains a potent topoisomerase I inhibitor payload (Ed-04). The aim of PANKU-Breast01 was to compare the efficacy and safety of iza-bren versus chemotherapy in patients with pretreated HR+/HER2- breast cancer.<h4>Methods</h4>In this randomised, phase 3, open-label, multicentre study in 72 hospitals in China, patients with HR+/HER2- breast cancer who had disease progression after endocrine therapy, cyclin-dependent kinase (CDK) 4/6 inhibitors, and one or two lines of chemotherapy were randomly assigned (1:1) to iza-bren (2·5 mg/kg, administered by intravenous infusion on day 1 and day 8 every 3 weeks) or physician's choice chemotherapy (eribulin, 1·4 mg/m<sup>2</sup>; vinorelbine, 25 mg/m<sup>2</sup>; or gemcitabine, 1000 mg/m<sup>2</sup>, all administered by intravenous infusion on day 1 and day 8 every 3 weeks; or capecitabine, 1000-1250 mg/m<sup>2</sup>, administered orally twice daily on days 1-14 of each 3-week cycle). Stratified block randomisation was performed by an independent vendor using an interactive web response system, with stratification factors of number of previous lines of chemotherapy, CNS metastasis, and HER2 expression status. Patients, investigators, and site personnel involved in patient care and performing trial procedures were not masked to treatment assignment. The primary endpoint was progression-free survival assessed by Blinded Independent Central Review per RECIST (version 1.1) in the modified intention-to-treat population, which consisted of all randomly assigned participants who had at least one dose of study drug. Median progression-free survival was estimated using the Kaplan-Meier method and hazard ratios (HRs) were calculated using a stratified Cox proportional hazards model. The trial was registered at ClinicalTrials.gov, NCT06343948, and is closed to recruitment.<h4>Findings</h4>Between April 24, 2024, and June 6, 2025, 560 patients were screened, 177 were excluded, and 383 patients were randomly assigned and treated (190 were assigned to the iza-bren group and 193 were assigned to the chemotherapy group). All 383 participants in the study were female and of Chinese ethnicity. Median age was 54·0 years (IQR 47·0-60·0). At data cutoff (July 21, 2026), median follow-up for progression-free survival was 15·2 months (95% CI 13·9-16·8). Iza-bren significantly improved progression-free survival versus chemotherapy (log-rank p<0·0001), and median progression-free survival assessed by Blinded Independent Central Review was 8·2 months (95% CI 6·9-8·6) versus 4·0 months (2·9-4·3; HR 0·41 [95% CI 0·33-0·53]). Grade 3 or higher treatment-related adverse events occurred in 162 (85%) patients in the iza-bren group versus 127 (66%) patients in the chemotherapy group, and haematological adverse events were the most common in both groups. For the treatment of haematological adverse events, 138 (73%) patients in the iza-bren group received platelet-elevating agents, 28 (15%) patients in the iza-bren group received platelet transfusion, and 18 patients (10%) in the iza-bren group received a blood transfusion. Two patients in the iza-bren group died due to treatment-related adverse events.<h4>Interpretation</h4>Iza-bren showed a significant improvement in progression-free survival versus chemotherapy in patients with HR+/HER2- breast cancer previously treated with endocrine therapy, CDK4/6 inhibitors, and one or two lines of chemotherapy (including taxanes). The overall survival data were immature, and an overall survival benefit has not been established. These results suggest that iza-bren could be a new treatment option for this population in China; however, further evaluation of iza-bren in global populations and in earlier lines of therapy for HER2-negative breast cancer is warranted.<h4>Funding</h4>Baili-Bio (Chengdu) Pharmaceutical, CAMS Innovation Fund for Medical Sciences, Noncommunicable Chronic Diseases-National Science and Technology Major Project, National High Level Hospital Clinical Research Funding, and the National Natural Science Foundation of China.

### [Izalontamab brengitecan versus platinum-based chemotherapy in EGFR-mutated advanced non-squamous non-small-cell lung cancer after progression on third-generation EGFR tyrosine kinase inhibitors (PANKU-Lung01): a multicentre, randomised, open-label, phase 3 trial in China.](https://doi.org/10.1016/s0140-6736(26)02026-x)

- Source: `lancet_abstracts` · published `2026-10-26` · freshness `stale`
- Reading value: `pending` · status: `ineligible`
- Event: `seen` · identity: `pmid:42933461`

<h4>Background</h4>Izalontamab brengitecan (iza-bren) is a first-in-class bispecific antibody-drug conjugate targeting epidermal growth factor receptor (EGFR) and human epidermal growth factor receptor 3 (HER3) and has shown antitumour activity in EGFR-mutated non-small-cell lung cancer (NSCLC). We compared iza-bren with platinum-based chemotherapy in participants with EGFR-mutated advanced non-squamous NSCLC after progression on third-generation EGFR tyrosine kinase inhibitors (TKIs).<h4>Methods</h4>In this multicentre, randomised, open-label, phase 3 trial (PANKU-Lung01) conducted at 73 sites in China, eligible participants had EGFR-mutated locally advanced or metastatic non-squamous NSCLC, disease progression after third-generation EGFR TKIs, and had received no previous chemotherapy for advanced disease. Participants were randomly assigned (1:1) to receive iza-bren (2·5 mg/kg intravenously on days 1 and 8 of each 3-week cycle) or pemetrexed plus carboplatin or cisplatin for four to six cycles, with pemetrexed maintenance permitted in participants without progression. The primary endpoint was progression-free survival assessed by blinded independent central review (BICR). The primary analysis included all randomly assigned participants who received at least one dose of study treatment (modified intention-to-treat population). We report the prespecified final progression-free survival and interim overall survival analyses. This trial is registered with ClinicalTrials.gov, NCT06382116.<h4>Findings</h4>Between May 22, 2024, and Sept 1, 2025, we screened 654 participants. After 222 (34%) were excluded, 432 (66%) participants were randomly assigned to iza-bren (n=217 [50%]) or chemotherapy (n=215 [50%]); 422 (98%) received study treatment (216 [51%] in the iza-bren group and 206 [49%] in the chemotherapy group) and comprised the modified intention-to-treat population. At the data cutoff (July 15, 2026; median follow-up for progression-free survival 14·6 months [95% CI 12·9-16·7] in the iza-bren group vs 15·2 months [11·2-18·2] in the chemotherapy group), median BICR-assessed progression-free survival was 11·1 months (9·2-12·7) with iza-bren versus 5·7 months (5·5-5·9) with chemotherapy (hazard ratio [HR] 0·40 [95% CI 0·31-0·51]; one-sided p<0·0001). Treatment-related adverse events of grade 3 or worse occurred in 170 (79%) of 216 participants in the iza-bren group and 128 (62%) of 206 in the chemotherapy group (predominantly haematological); treatment-related adverse events led to permanent discontinuation of at least one study-treatment component in four (2%) participants in each group and treatment-related deaths occurred in one participant in each group. Treatment-related interstitial lung disease occurred in two (1%) participants in the iza-bren group and none in the chemotherapy group.<h4>Interpretation</h4>In participants with EGFR-mutated advanced non-squamous NSCLC after progression on third-generation EGFR TKIs, iza-bren significantly prolonged progression-free survival, although treatment-related adverse events of grade 3 or worse were more frequent than with chemotherapy. To our knowledge, these findings provide the first phase 3 evidence for a bispecific antibody-drug conjugate and support iza-bren as a potential treatment option in this population.<h4>Funding</h4>Baili-Bio (Chengdu) Pharmaceutical.

### [Ruzaltatug rezetecan versus chemotherapy for EGFR-mutated advanced non-small-cell lung cancer progressing after EGFR tyrosine kinase inhibitor therapy (HERON-Lung01): interim analysis of a randomised, open-label, phase 3 trial in China.](https://doi.org/10.1016/s0140-6736(26)01899-4)

<h4>Background</h4>Ruzaltatug rezetecan is a novel HER3-directed antibody-drug conjugate that has shown promising antitumour activity and manageable safety in pretreated EGFR-.mutated non-small-cell lung cancer (NSCLC) in a phase 1 trial. We aimed to compare the efficacy and safety of ruzaltatug rezetecan with chemotherapy in patients with EGFR-mutated advanced NSCLC that had progressed after EGFR tyrosine kinase inhibitor (TKI) therapy.<h4>Methods</h4>HERON-Lung01 is a randomised, open-label, phase 3 trial conducted at 62 sites in China. Eligible patients were aged 18-75 years, with locally advanced or metastatic non-squamous NSCLC harbouring an EGFR activating mutation (exon 19 deletion or L858R mutation), and had progressed during or after EGFR-TKI therapy. Patients were randomly assigned (1:1) to receive ruzaltatug rezetecan 8·0 mg/kg or chemotherapy comprising pemetrexed 500 mg/m<sup>2</sup> plus carboplatin (area under the curve 5) or cisplatin 75 mg/m<sup>2</sup> for four cycles, followed by maintenance pemetrexed. All treatments were administered intravenously on day 1 of each 3-week cycle. Block randomisation was stratified by presence of brain metastases, EGFR mutation subtype, and use of a first-line third-generation EGFR-TKI. The primary endpoint was progression-free survival (PFS), as assessed by blinded independent central review (BICR), in the intention-to-treat population. This trial is registered with ClinicalTrials.gov (NCT06671379). Recruitment is complete, with the trial ongoing.<h4>Findings</h4>Between Nov 29, 2024, and Jan 29, 2026, 498 patients were randomly assigned to receive ruzaltatug rezetecan (n=250) or chemotherapy (n=248). The median age was 60·0 years (IQR 54·0-68·0); 276 (55%) patients were female and 222 (45%) male. At the prespecified interim PFS analysis, median follow-up was 7·7 months (95% CI 7·1-8·3). BICR-assessed PFS was significantly longer with ruzaltatug rezetecan than with chemotherapy (median 8·3 months vs 5·4 months; hazard ratio 0·47 [95% CI 0·36-0·60]; one-sided p<0·0001). Grade 3 or worse treatment-related adverse events (TRAEs) occurred in 101 (41%) of 248 patients receiving ruzaltatug rezetecan and 93 (39%) of 239 patients receiving chemotherapy, most commonly decreased neutrophil count (64 [26%] of 248 patients vs 46 [19%] of 239 patients), decreased white blood cell count (29 [12%] vs 29 [12%]), and anaemia (15 [6%] vs 26 [11%]). TRAEs led to treatment discontinuation in three (1%) patients in the ruzaltatug rezetecan group and three (1%) in the chemotherapy group. One death (unknown cause) in the ruzaltatug rezetecan group was considered by the investigator to be possibly treatment related.<h4>Interpretation</h4>Ruzaltatug rezetecan significantly prolonged PFS compared with chemotherapy, with a manageable safety profile, in EGFR-TKI-resistant, EGFR-mutated advanced NSCLC. These data support ruzaltatug rezetecan as a potential treatment option in this setting.<h4>Funding</h4>Jiangsu Hengrui Pharmaceuticals.

### [Sacituzumab tirumotecan versus treatment of physician's choice in previously treated endometrial cancer (TroFuse-005/ENGOT-en23/GOG-3095): a randomised, open-label, phase 3 trial.](https://doi.org/10.1016/s0140-6736(26)02099-4)

- Source: `lancet_abstracts` · published `2026-10-26` · freshness `stale`
- Reading value: `pending` · status: `ineligible`
- Event: `seen` · identity: `pmid:42933460`

<h4>Background</h4>Therapeutic options for patients with endometrial cancer who progress after platinum-based chemotherapy and immunotherapy are scarce, and prognosis is poor. Sacituzumab tirumotecan (sac-TMT) is a trophoblast cell-surface antigen 2-directed antibody-drug conjugate with a unique, bifunctional linker that maximises delivery of a belotecan-derived topoisomerase I inhibitor payload to tumour cells. This study aimed to compare sac-TMT with the treatment of physician's choice (TPC) in patients with endometrial cancer who had been previously treated with platinum-based chemotherapy and immunotherapy.<h4>Methods</h4>In this randomised, open-label, phase 3 trial, patients were recruited from 242 gynaecological oncology centres across 31 countries and regions. Participating centres were selected based on established expertise in gynaecological oncology and an adequate volume of potentially eligible patients. Eligible patients were female and aged 18 years or older with histologically confirmed advanced or recurrent endometrial carcinoma or carcinosarcoma, who had received up to three previous lines of therapy, including systemic platinum-based chemotherapy and anti-programmed death 1 or anti-programmed death ligand 1 therapy. Patients were randomly assigned (1:1) via central interactive response technology to receive intravenous sac-TMT (4 mg/kg on days 1 and 15 of each 28-day cycle) or TPC (intravenous doxorubicin 60 mg/m<sup>2</sup> every 3 weeks or intravenous paclitaxel 80 mg/m<sup>2</sup> on days 1, 8, and 15 of each 28-day cycle). Investigators and patients were not masked to treatment; radiologists performing blinded independent central review were fully masked. Randomisation was stratified by mismatch repair status (deficient vs proficient), trophoblast cell-surface antigen 2 expression by immunohistochemistry (TROP2 EPR20043 Formulation Locked Assay [Roche Tissue Diagnostics, Tucson, AZ, USA]; low [0% to <50% of tumour cells with moderate (2+) or strong (3+) membrane staining intensity] vs medium-high [≥50% tumour cells with moderate (2+) or strong (3+) membrane staining intensity]), number of previous lines of systemic therapy (≤2 vs 3), and baseline disease status per Response Evaluation Criteria in Solid Tumors (version 1.1) by blinded independent central review (measurable vs non-measurable). Dual primary endpoints were progression-free survival per Response Evaluation Criteria in Solid Tumors (version 1.1) assessed by blinded independent central review and overall survival, analysed in the intention-to-treat population. The data cutoff date was March 23, 2026. Progression-free survival and overall survival were evaluated as dual primary endpoints using a graphical multiple-testing approach with prespecified alpha allocation. Progression-free survival was assessed every 8 weeks through to week 56, then every 12 weeks thereafter; overall survival was assessed continuously. The safety analysis included all randomly assigned patients who received at least one dose of study treatment. For progression-free survival, missing radiographic assessments were handled using prespecified censoring rules; no imputation was performed for missing overall survival or safety data. The trial was registered on ClinicalTrials.gov (NCT06132958) and is ongoing.<h4>Findings</h4>Between Jan 5, 2024, and Sept 17, 2025, 776 patients were randomly assigned to sac-TMT (n=386) or TPC (n=390). 384 (99%) of 386 patients in the sac-TMT group and 367 (94%) of 390 in the TPC group received at least one dose of study treatment. At data cutoff, median follow-up was 14·0 months (IQR 11·0-16·9). Median progression-free survival was 7·2 months (95% CI 5·6-7·6) in the sac-TMT group and 3·9 months (3·7-5·3) in the TPC group (hazard ratio 0·59 [95% CI 0·50-0·70]; p<0·0001) and median overall survival was 19·0 months (16·1-not reached) in the sac-TMT group and 12·1 months (11·0-15·2) in the TPC group (0·68 [0·55-0·84]; p=0·0001). 265 (69%) of 384 patients in the sac-TMT group and 210 (57%) of 367 in the TPC group had a grade 3 or higher adverse event. Serious adverse events were reported in 133 (35%) patients in the sac-TMT group and 117 (32%) patients in the TPC group. Treatment-related adverse events led to death in three (<1%) patients in the sac-TMT group and three (<1%) in the TPC group.<h4>Interpretation</h4>Sac-TMT significantly improved progression-free survival and overall survival versus TPC in patients with advanced or recurrent endometrial cancer previously treated with platinum-based chemotherapy and immunotherapy. These findings support sac-TMT as a new standard of care for patients with endometrial cancer who have received chemotherapy and immunotherapy and have few effective treatment options.<h4>Funding</h4>Merck Sharp & Dohme.

### [Single Intranasal Administration of the Psychedelic Mebufotenin for Treatment-Resistant Depression: A Randomized Clinical Trial.](https://doi.org/10.1001/jama.2026.18380)

- Source: `jama_abstracts` · published `2026-10-26` · freshness `stale`
- Reading value: `pending` · status: `ineligible`
- Event: `seen` · identity: `pmid:42931733`

<h4>Importance</h4>Psychedelics represent a potential breakthrough for treatment-resistant depression; however, the prolonged in-clinic monitoring required for compounds such as psilocybin and lysergic acid diethylamide is a logistical hurdle to widespread use. Intranasal mebufotenin (5-methoxy-N,N-dimethyltryptamine), a 5-hydroxytryptamine 1A (5-HT1A) and 5-HT2A agonist associated with brief psychedelic experiences, may address this limitation.<h4>Objective</h4>To evaluate the safety, efficacy, and time to discharge readiness following a single dose of intranasal mebufotenin benzoate in adults with treatment-resistant depression.<h4>Design, setting, and participants</h4>Phase 2b, randomized, double-blind trial with an 8-week blinded follow-up conducted between September 2023 and April 2025 at 38 sites in Australia, Europe, and the US. Participants were aged 18 to 75 years with moderate to severe major depressive disorder nonresponsive to 2 to 5 antidepressant trials in the current episode. The last date of follow-up was April 28, 2025.<h4>Interventions</h4>After antidepressant washout as needed, participants were randomly assigned to receive a single dose of intranasal mebufotenin benzoate, 8 mg (n = 46), 12 mg (n = 73), or 0.3 mg (a low-dose comparator; n = 74), alongside psychological support.<h4>Main outcomes and measures</h4>The primary end point was total score change on the Montgomery-Åsberg Depression Rating Scale (MADRS; score range, 0-60; higher scores indicate more severe depression) from baseline to day 29 in the 12-mg vs 0.3-mg dose groups. Eighteen secondary end points were assessed for the 12-mg and 8-mg vs 0.3-mg doses. Discharge readiness was assessed starting 90 minutes after dosing.<h4>Results</h4>Among the 193 included participants, the mean age was 41 (SD, 11.1) years and 59.1% were female. From baseline to day 29, treatment with 12-mg intranasal mebufotenin benzoate vs the 0.3-mg dose (the primary end point) was associated with significantly reduced MADRS scores (mean score change, -11.1 [SD, 11.6] vs -5.5 [SD, 9.9], respectively; mean difference, -5.3 [95% CI, -8.9 to -1.8]; P = .003). The 8-mg dose also led to reduced scores from baseline to day 29 (mean score change, -12.5 [SD, 12.7]) vs the 0.3-mg dose (mean difference, -6.2 [95% CI, -10.2 to -2.1]; P = .003). MADRS reductions with the 8-mg and 12-mg doses were similar and significantly greater at all other time points from 2 to 57 days post dose vs the 0.3-mg dose. Day 29 response rates were 35%, 34%, and 12% and remission rates were 26%, 18%, and 8% in the 8-mg, 12-mg, and 0.3-mg dose groups, respectively. Treatment-emergent adverse events were reported in 76%, 85%, and 73% of participants in the 8-mg, 12-mg, and 0.3-mg dose groups, respectively; most events (99.7%) were mild or moderate. Overall, most participants were deemed ready for discharge within 2 hours post dose (85% for 8 mg and 78% for 12 mg).<h4>Conclusions and relevance</h4>In adults with treatment-resistant depression, single doses of intranasal mebufotenin benzoate were well tolerated and produced rapid, robust, and durable reductions in depressive symptoms. Intranasal mebufotenin benzoate may improve treatment access and convenience, requiring a shorter monitoring period and fewer clinic visits compared with psilocybin and esketamine, respectively.<h4>Trial registration</h4>ClinicalTrials.gov Identifier: NCT05870540.

### [Comparative effectiveness of three splints for rehabilitation after finger flexor tendon repair (FIRST): randomised controlled trial.](https://doi.org/10.1136/bmj-2026-100953)

- Source: `bmj_abstracts` · published `2026-10-27` · freshness `stale`
- Reading value: `pending` · status: `ineligible`
- Event: `seen` · identity: `pmid:42946338`

<h4>Objective</h4>To compare the clinical effectiveness of three splints after repair of zone 1 and 2 flexor tendons: forearm based (long), Manchester short (short), and relative motion flexion (mini) splints.<h4>Design</h4>Prospective, randomised, parallel group, pragmatic, superiority, clinical trial.<h4>Setting</h4>26 UK hospitals.<h4>Participants</h4>430 participants (aged ≥16 years) requiring rehabilitation after primary repair of zone 1 and 2 flexor tendons in one or two fingers of the hand according to British Society for Surgery of the Hand guidelines.<h4>Interventions</h4>Participants were randomly assigned 1:1:1 to the long, short, or mini splint. Splints were worn continuously for five weeks and intermittently for a further week at night and in vulnerable situations such as public environments. Masking participants was not possible owing to the nature of the intervention.<h4>Main outcome measures</h4>The primary outcome was the mean post-randomisation score on the patient-rated wrist and hand evaluation (PRWHE) instrument at 6, 12, 26, and 52 weeks, analysed by intention to treat.<h4>Results</h4>430 participants were randomised to the long splint (n=143), short splint (n=140), or mini splint (n=147) and 403 were included in the intention-to-treat analysis. Adherence, defined as wearing the splint for a minimum of five weeks, was 83% (118/143) for the long splint, 76% (106/140) for the short splint, and 78% (115/147) for the mini splint. Mean post-randomisation PRWHE scores were 24.5 (standard deviation 15.5) in the long splint group, 23.5 (16.7) in the short splint group, and 22.4 (15.0) in the mini splint group, with adjusted between group differences of 0.3 (98.3% confidence interval -4.0 to 4.6, P=0.88) for the long versus short splint, 2.3 (-1.9 to 6.5, P=0.19) for the long versus mini splint, and 2.0 (-2.2 to 6.3, P=0.25) for the short versus mini splint. Serious adverse event rates occurred in 10% (14/143) of participants in the long splint group, 11% (15/140) in the short splint group, and 11% (16/147) in the mini splint group.<h4>Conclusion</h4>This trial found no statistically significant difference in clinical effectiveness between the long, short, and mini splint. Apart from some small differences, secondary outcomes were comparable across all three groups.<h4>Trial registration</h4>ISRCTN Registry ISRCTN10236011.

### [Effectiveness of Wrist-Worn Wearable–Guided Cardiac Rehabilitation Delivered Through Fully Remote or Hybrid Programs in Patients With Cardiovascular Disease: Systematic Review and Meta-Analysis of Randomized Controlled Trials](https://mhealth.jmir.org/2026/1/e101306)

- Source: `jmir_mhealth` · published `2026-10-27` · freshness `stale`
- Reading value: `pending` · status: `ineligible`
- Event: `seen` · identity: `url:https://mhealth.jmir.org/2026/1/e101306`

Background: Cardiovascular disease (CVD) remains the leading cause of death worldwide, yet participation in cardiac rehabilitation (CR) remains suboptimal. Wrist-worn wearables may support fully remote or hybrid CR through activity monitoring and individualized feedback, but the effectiveness and consistency of benefits remain uncertain. Objective: This study aimed to evaluate the effectiveness of wrist-worn wearable–guided CR delivered through fully remote or hybrid programs initiated during phase II or III CR in patients with CVD. Methods: A total of 8 databases were searched for randomized controlled trials published from January 1, 2015, through August 7, 2026. Eligible trials enrolled adults with CVD and integrated a wrist-worn wearable into fully remote or hybrid CR. Studies focused primarily on heart failure or using wearables only for outcome measurement were excluded. Primary outcomes were peak oxygen consumption (VO<sub>2</sub>), 6-minute walk distance (6MWD), and daily steps. Random-effects meta-analyses used mean differences (MDs), except for quality of life, which used standardized MDs. Between-study variance was estimated using the DerSimonian-Laird method, pooled-effect CIs used the Knapp-Hartung adjustment, and 95% prediction intervals (PIs) used the Nagashima-Noma-Furukawa parametric bootstrap method. Risk of bias was assessed with Cochrane Risk of Bias 2. Subgroup and univariable meta-regression analyses explored study and intervention characteristics. Results: A total of 18 trials involving 1993 randomized participants, reported in 20 publications, were included; 17 contributed to meta-analysis. Pooled estimates favored wearable-guided CR for peak VO<sub>2</sub> (10 studies; 591 participants; MD 1.69, 95% CI 0.46-2.92, 95% PI –2.21 to 5.39 mL/kg/min), 6MWD (9 studies; 1094 participants; MD 31.03, 95% CI 7.66-53.92, 95% PI –62.11 to 123.18 m), and daily steps (8 studies; 474 participants; MD 1062.8, 95% CI 383.5-1825.0, 95% PI –503.0 to 2860.0 steps/day). All 3 PIs included the null, indicating uncertainty about consistency across future comparable settings. No clear pooled effects were observed for anaerobic threshold, BMI, waist circumference, grip strength, or quality of life. Subgroup differences were not statistically significant. In univariable meta-regression, a higher proportion of female participants was associated with greater peak VO<sub>2</sub> improvement, although this aggregate-level finding was exploratory. Conclusions: Wrist-worn wearable–guided CR delivered through fully remote or hybrid programs may improve average exercise capacity and physical activity in patients with CVD. However, PIs included the null, indicating uncertainty about consistency across future settings. These programs may extend activity monitoring, individualized feedback, and behavioral support beyond center-based CR, but their multicomponent nature prevents attributing effects to wearables alone. Larger, rigorously designed trials with standardized protocols and outcomes should identify the patients and program components most likely to benefit. Future research should also evaluate long-term adherence, safety, equity, and implementation within routine multidisciplinary CR across diverse health systems.

<h4>Background</h4>Ambient artificial intelligence (AI) medical scribes may reduce documentation burden and clinician burnout. Whether use intensity drives benefits remains uncertain.<h4>Objective</h4>To evaluate the association between AI scribe (Heidi) enrollment and documentation burden and whether changes differ by use intensity.<h4>Design</h4>Retrospective quality improvement study using an interrupted time series (ITS) indexed on each clinician's enrollment month adjusted for patient demographics and visit complexity. A secondary 3-group ITS compared enrolled nonusers, low users, and high users.<h4>Setting</h4>14-hospital health system in Boston, Massachusetts.<h4>Participants</h4>902 outpatient clinicians enrolled in Heidi; 605 users and 297 enrolled nonusers.<h4>Intervention</h4>Heidi enrollment after system-wide implementation beginning January 2025.<h4>Measurements</h4>The primary outcome was note-active time. Secondary outcomes included same-day and after-hours note closures, note length, and work relative value units (wRVUs).<h4>Results</h4>1 637 227 encounters had notes authored by enrolled clinicians between 1 July 2024 and 31 January 2026. Enrollment was associated with an immediate reduction in note-active time (-1.21 minutes [95% CI, -1.74 to -0.68 minutes]), with larger reductions among high users (-2.50 minutes [CI, -3.55 to -1.45 minutes]) than nonusers (difference, -2.20 minutes [CI, -3.46 to -0.95 minutes]) and low users (difference, -1.72 minutes [CI, -3.03 to -0.40 minutes]). High users had greater increases in note length than nonusers (difference, +170.90 characters [CI, 21.07 to 320.70 characters]) and low users (difference, +270.70 characters [CI, 131.04 to 410.36 characters]). No differences were observed in same-day closure, after-hours closure, or wRVUs.<h4>Limitation</h4>Observational design with potential unmeasured confounding; inability to distinguish AI-scribed from clinician-authored notes postenrollment.<h4>Conclusion</h4>Heidi enrollment was associated with reductions in documentation time and increases in note length, concentrated among high users. Enrollment was not associated with improvements in same-day, after-hours note closures, or wRVUs.<h4>Primary funding source</h4>None.

<h4>Background</h4>Although international guidelines recommend intrauterine insemination (IUI) with ovarian stimulation as first-line treatment for unexplained infertility, evidence from comparisons of this method with in vitro fertilization (IVF) is limited.<h4>Methods</h4>We conducted a sequential, multicenter, randomized, controlled trial involving couples with unexplained infertility. Stage 1 was a noninferiority comparison between IUI with ovarian stimulation (up to four cycles) and IVF (one complete cycle, with transfer of all embryos). After 7 months, couples without live birth or ongoing pregnancy could proceed to stage 2: couples in the IUI group could undergo up to two IVF cycles, and those in the IVF group could undergo one further IVF cycle. The primary outcome was the cumulative live-birth rate resulting from stage 1. Couples who were in stage 1 during lockdowns due to coronavirus disease 2019 were omitted from the analysis of the primary outcome. A secondary outcome was the cumulative live-birth rate resulting from stages 1 and 2. Live births were included in analyses if day 1 of the treatment cycle that led to pregnancy or day 1 of the last menstrual period before conception occurred within 7 months (stage 1) or 20 months (stage 2) after randomization. The noninferiority margin for the primary outcome was a between-group difference of 10 percentage points.<h4>Results</h4>A total of 737 couples underwent randomization: 365 couples were assigned to undergo IUI with ovarian stimulation before IVF, and 372 were assigned to undergo first-line IVF (1 couple in the IVF group withdrew consent for the use of data). The primary-outcome analysis included 585 couples (288 in the IUI group and 297 in the IVF group). The cumulative live-birth rate from stage 1 was 31% (89 births among 288 couples) with IUI with ovarian stimulation and 55% (164 births among 297 couples) with IVF (adjusted risk difference, -24 percentage points; 95% confidence interval [CI], -31 to -16). The cumulative live-birth rate from stages 1 and 2 among all the couples who had undergone randomization and provided consent was 70% (257 births among 365 couples) and 71% (265 births among 371 couples), respectively (adjusted risk difference, 0 percentage points; 99% CI, -9 to 8). Overall, serious adverse events occurred in 6 couples in the IUI group and in 14 couples in the IVF group; most serious adverse events were related to oocyte retrieval or hyperstimulation.<h4>Conclusions</h4>In couples with unexplained infertility, treatment with four cycles of IUI with ovarian stimulation was not noninferior to treatment with one complete cycle of IVF with respect to the cumulative live-birth rate. (Funded by the Health Research Council of New Zealand and others; FIIX Australian New Zealand Clinical Trials Registry number, ACTRN12619001003167.).

<h4>Background</h4>Randomized clinical trials (RCTs) have compared laparoscopic liver resection (LLR) and open liver resection (OLR) for colorectal liver metastases (CRLM); however, long-term outcomes and subgroup effects have not been comprehensively evaluated.<h4>Purpose</h4>To compare postoperative and long-term oncologic outcomes after LLR versus OLR for CRLM using individual participant data from RCTs.<h4>Data sources</h4>PubMed, Embase, and Cochrane Central Register of Controlled Trials were searched from database inception through March 2025 for RCTs comparing LLR and OLR for CRLM.<h4>Study selection</h4>Eligible studies were RCTs comparing LLR and OLR for CRLM.<h4>Data extraction</h4>Individual participant data, including unpublished data, were obtained from trial investigators of all 4 published RCTs.<h4>Data synthesis</h4>Analyses used a modified intention-to-treat approach and 1-stage individual participant data meta-analysis (IPDMA) with trial-adjusted regression models and g-computation to estimate standardized treatment effects across outcomes, with 95% CIs obtained by nonparametric bootstrap resampling. Prespecified subgroup analyses assessed heterogeneity of treatment effect for the primary outcome. A total of 761 patients were included (laparoscopic, <i>n </i>= 384; open, <i>n </i>= 377). Postoperative morbidity was lower in patients assigned to laparoscopic surgery compared with open surgery (19% vs. 27%). Among patients who received preoperative systemic therapy, morbidity did not differ between groups (25% vs. 24%). Ninety-day mortality was 1.8% after LLR and 0.8% after OLR. After a median follow-up of 93 months, overall survival and disease-free survival did not differ between groups.<h4>Limitation</h4>Analyses were limited to variables available across all studies.<h4>Conclusion</h4>In this IPDMA of all published RCTs in the field, LLR for CRLM was associated with lower postoperative morbidity than OLR, with similar long-term oncologic outcomes.<h4>Primary funding source</h4>None. (PROSPERO: CRD42024502655).

### [Obesity and Cancer Risk in Young Adults in Korea : A Nationwide Health Screening Cohort Study.](https://doi.org/10.7326/annals-25-02612)

<h4>Background</h4>Obesity and early-onset cancer are both increasing among young adults, yet evidence remains limited.<h4>Objective</h4>To assess associations between obesity and early-onset cancer risk in young adults.<h4>Design</h4>Nationwide population-based cohort study.<h4>Setting</h4>Korean National Health Insurance Service database, 2009 to 2021.<h4>Patients</h4>6 313 338 adults aged 20 to 39 years without prior cancer, from a national health screening program.<h4>Measurements</h4>Body mass index (BMI) was categorized into 5 groups (underweight to class II obesity) according to Asian-specific criteria. The outcome was incident cancer.<h4>Results</h4>During a mean follow-up of 11.2 years, 151 893 cancer cases occurred. Ten-year cumulative incidence ranged from 1.84% (95% CI, 1.83% to 1.86%) in the normal-weight group to 2.24% (CI, 2.22% to 2.27%) in class I obesity and 2.62% (CI, 2.56% to 2.68%) in class II obesity, with absolute risk differences of 0.40% (CI, 0.39% to 0.41%) and 0.78% (CI, 0.73% to 0.82%). The relative association was more pronounced in men, and absolute risk differences were greater in women. In combined BMI-waist circumference analyses, people with class I obesity based on BMI and with abdominal obesity had higher 10-year cumulative incidence (2.45% [CI, 2.41% to 2.50%]) than those without abdominal obesity (2.13% [CI, 2.10% to 2.16%]), exceeding that of those with class II obesity without abdominal obesity (2.20% [CI, 2.03% to 2.36%]). The highest cumulative incidence was observed for thyroid, endometrial, and ovarian cancer. Obesity was not associated with higher risk for breast cancer diagnosed by age 50 years.<h4>Limitations</h4>Observational design; nonrepresentative sample limited to screening participants; and unmeasured confounding, including smoking burden.<h4>Conclusion</h4>Obesity in young Korean adults, measured by BMI and waist circumference, is associated with higher early-onset cancer risk persisting into midlife, with patterns varying by cancer type. Early weight management may be important for prevention.<h4>Primary funding source</h4>None.

### [Automation of Living Clinical Practice Guideline Development: Scoping Review](https://ai.jmir.org/2026/1/e103273/)

- Source: `jmir_ai` · published `2026-10-28` · freshness `stale`
- Reading value: `pending` · status: `ineligible`
- Event: `seen` · identity: `url:https://ai.jmir.org/2026/1/e103273/`

&lt;strong&gt;Background:&lt;/strong&gt; Clinical practice guidelines (CPGs) translate research evidence into actionable recommendations, but their development faces mounting challenges. With dozens of trials and systematic reviews published daily, the traditional 18- to 24-month development cycle struggles to incorporate emerging evidence, and manual screening, synthesis, and quality assessment consume thousands of person-hours per guideline. The resulting knowledge translation pipeline is slow, inconsistent, and quickly outdated. &lt;strong&gt;Objective:&lt;/strong&gt; This review aimed to chart the computational methods empirically tested for automating CPG development, the tasks they address, and their evaluation measures, and to map where that evidence is concentrated and where it is absent for living guidelines. &lt;strong&gt;Methods:&lt;/strong&gt; The review followed the Joanna Briggs Institute scoping review framework and PRISMA-ScR (Preferred Reporting Items for Systematic Reviews and Meta-Analyses extension for Scoping Reviews) guidance. We searched PubMed and Scopus for studies published between January 2001 and December 2025, supplemented by backward and forward citation searching. Eligible studies used automated methods for a guideline development task, reported original research with empirical testing, and gave sufficient methodological detail. Two reviewers independently screened records and extracted data. Findings were charted across 4 pillars: retrieval, extraction, encoding, and evaluation. &lt;strong&gt;Results:&lt;/strong&gt; A total of 47 studies met eligibility criteria, spanning 2001 to 2025, with a marked increase after 2015 and a surge in large language model (LLM) work in 2024-2025. Pillar counts (nonexclusive) were as follows: retrieval, 6; extraction, 15; encoding, 26; and evaluation, 17. In retrieval pillar, query expansion achieved 80% recall, while semantic search tools traded recall for specificity (98%). In extraction, rule-based natural language processing achieved an F-measure of 81.96% for structured clinical actions and 71.75% for patient states, and hybrid ontology methods covered 86% of eligibility criteria; LLMs performed well on structured fields but degraded on interpretive content. In encoding, approaches progressed from formalisms such as Guideline Interchange Format version 3 (GLIF3) and guideline elements model toward Fast Healthcare Interoperability Resources (FHIR)–based representation; semiautomated tools assisted guideline encoding, and logic verification detected inconsistencies and coverage gaps missed by human reviewers. In evaluation, LLM appraisers assigned higher Appraisal of Guidelines for Research and Evaluation II (AGREE II) scores than human appraisers, by a mean difference of 12.5% in one study (95% limits of agreement –30.6% to 55.5%; intraclass correlation coefficient 0.753) and by 22 to 60 percentage points across domains in another. &lt;strong&gt;Conclusions:&lt;/strong&gt; Automation shows promise for bounded, well-structured guideline development tasks, while robust evidence for end-to-end, production-scale living guideline pipelines remains limited. No included study demonstrated a validated pipeline connecting all 4 pillars in an operating guideline program, and evidence on clinical safety, workflow integration, and cost-effectiveness is largely absent. Realizing living guidelines will require interoperability standards such as FHIR, and evaluation in real guideline production rather than retrospective benchmarks. <img src="https://jmir-production.s3.us-east-2.amazonaws.com/thumbs/2a6d269cf8e2d48cbe94df1b7e290662" />

### [Calendar Year (CY) 2027 End-Stage Renal Disease (ESRD) Prospective Payment System Final Rule — CMS-1846-F](https://www.cms.gov/newsroom/fact-sheets/calendar-year-cy-2027-end-stage-renal-disease-esrd-prospective-payment-system-final-rule-cms-1846-f)

- Source: `cms_fact_sheets` · published `2026-10-28` · freshness `stale`
- Reading value: `pending` · status: `ineligible`
- Event: `seen` · identity: `url:https://www.cms.gov/newsroom/fact-sheets/calendar-year-cy-2027-end-stage-renal-disease-esrd-prospective-payment-system-final-rule-cms-1846-f`

Calendar Year (CY) 2027 End-Stage Renal Disease (ESRD) Prospective Payment System Final Rule — CMS-1846-F Combating kidney disease starts with prevention, early detection, and timely treatment to help protect kidney health and slow disease progression. For Americans who depend on dialysis, high-quality care is vital to their health and daily lives. The Centers for Medicare & Medicaid Services (CMS) is committed to improving how our kidney care system serves patients by supporting access to lifesaving treatment so more Americans can live longer, healthier lives. CMS is advancing its goals for dialysis access and quality through the calendar year (CY) 2027 Medicare End-Stage Renal Disease (ESRD) Prospective Payment System (PPS) final rule. On Oct. 28, 2026, CMS issued a final rule to update payment rates and policies under the ESRD PPS for renal dialysis services furnished to Medicare beneficiaries on or after Jan. 1, 2027. This rule also finalizes updates to the acute kidney injury (AKI) dialysis payment rate for renal dialysis services furnished by ESRD facilities for calendar year (CY) 2027 and updates requirements for the ESRD Quality Incentive Program (QIP). For CY 2027, CMS will increase the ESRD PPS base rate to $297.52, which CMS expects will increase total payments to all ESRD facilities, both freestanding and hospital-based, by approximately 2.7%. This amount includes a $12.85 increase to account for the incorporation of phosphate binders into the ESRD PPS base rate. The CY 2027 ESRD PPS final rule also finalizes changes to the low-volume payment adjustment, changes to the payment adjustments for pediatric patients, an increase to the home and self-dialysis training add-on payment adjustment amount, and modifications to the transitional drug add-on payment adjustment (TDAPA), and modifications to the post-TDAPA add-on payment adjustment policies. Beginning with Payment Year (PY) 2029, CMS is replacing the Hypercalcemia reporting measure with the Facility-Level Percentage of Chronic Hyperphosphatemia in Dialysis Patients (Hyperphosphatemia) clinical measure and removing two reporting measures from the ESRD QIP. CMS is also updating the National Healthcare Safety Network (NHSN) Bloodstream Infection (BSI) in Hemodialysis Patients clinical measure national baseline data and risk-adjustment methodology. Additionally, CMS is updating the measure domains and the domain and measure weights used to calculate the total performance score. CMS also summarizes input received on a request for information on the potential inclusion of the Dialysis Facility Discussion of Patient Life Goals (D-PaLS) Patient-Reported Outcome Performance measure (PRO-PM) in the ESRD QIP. Final Updates to the ESRD PPS for CY 2027 The ESRD PPS provides a bundled, per-treatment payment to ESRD facilities that includes all renal dialysis services furnished for outpatient maintenance dialysis, including drugs and biological products. Additionally, the bundled payment includes all other renal dialysis items and services that were formerly separately payable under previous payment methodologies. The bundled payment rate is case-mix adjusted for several factors relating to patient characteristics. There are also facility-level adjustments for low patient volume, for ESRD facilities in rural areas, and for ESRD facilities in certain noncontiguous states and territories, as well as a wage index adjustment. When applicable, the bundled payment rate also includes a training payment for home and self-dialysis modalities; an outlier payment for high-cost patients; add-on payment adjustments for certain drugs, equipment, and supplies; and a pediatric ESRD add-on payment adjustment (through 2026). Annual Update to the ESRD PPS Base Rate Under the ESRD PPS for CY 2027, Medicare expects to pay $7.0 billion to approximately 7,600 ESRD facilities for furnishing renal dialysis services. The final CY 2027 ESRD PPS base rate is $297.52, which is an increase of $15.81 from the current CY 2026 base rate of $281.71. This amount includes $12.85 to reflect the inclusion of phosphate binders into the ESRD PPS base rate. Additionally, this amount reflects the application of the final wage index budget neutrality adjustment factor (1.00260), the final CY 2027 ESRD Bundled market basket update of 1.9%, and a budget neutrality factor of 0.98864 for the budget neutral changes to low-volume, pediatric, and home and self-dialysis training payment adjustments, equaling $297.52 [ ($281.71 + $12.85) x 1.00260 x 0.98864) x 1.019 = $297.52]. CMS projects that the CY 2027 updates will increase the total payments to all ESRD facilities by 2.7% compared with CY 2026. Hospital-based and freestanding ESRD facilities are both projected to have an estimated 2.7% increase in total payments. Wage Index Update CMS is making routine annual updates to the ESRD PPS-specific wage index that is used to adjust ESRD PPS payments for geographic differences in area wages. The ESRD PPS wage index methodology, finalized in the CY 2025 ESRD PPS final rule, combines data from the Bureau of Labor Statistics (BLS) Occupational Employment & Wage Statistics (OEWS) program and freestanding ESRD facility cost reports to produce the ESRD PPS-specific wage index. We are updating the ESRD PPS wage index for CY 2027 to use the latest available BLS OEWS data. CMS will continue to apply the wage-index floor of 0.6000 and a 5 % cap on wage-index decreases from the prior year, as finalized in the CY 2023 ESRD PPS final rule. Updates to the Outlier Policy CMS is making routine updates to the fixed dollar loss (FDL) and Medicare allowable payment (MAP) amounts for CY 2027. For pediatric beneficiaries, the final FDL amount will increase from $162.43 to $229.49, and the final MAP amount will increase from $50.19 to $63.27 as compared to CY 2026 values. For adult beneficiaries, the final FDL amount will increase from $14.80 to $90.60, and the final MAP amount will increase from $23.68 to $41.83. These increases are primarily attributable to the projected utilization of new drugs that will be ESRD outlier services in CY 2027. Addition to the ESRD PPS Base Rate for the Inclusion of Phosphate Binders CMS is finalizing its proposal to permanently increase the ESRD PPS base rate to account for the costs of phosphate binders, marking the completion of the incorporation of phosphate binders into the bundled payment, which began Jan. 1, 2025. This additional amount of $12.85 is based on the most recent average sales price (ASP) data for each of the six types of phosphate binders and on utilization during the TDAPA period, and includes operational costs equal to 6% of ASP. Low-Volume Payment Adjustment CMS is finalizing its proposal to increase the Low-Volume Payment Adjustment (LVPA) volume threshold from 4,000 to 8,000 treatments. This change will also expand the number of LVPA tiers from two to six. ESRD facilities with fewer than 3,000 median treatments over the past three cost reporting years will receive the greatest payment adjuster and ESRD facilities with between 7,000 and 7,999 median treatments will receive the smallest adjuster. We are also finalizing an extension to the attestation deadline for payment year 2027. ESRD facilities may attest that they meet the requirements of the LVPA up to Dec. 31, 2026, to receive the LVPA for CY 2027. This budget-neutral change will reduce the ESRD PPS base rate by approximately 1.1% (~$3). The updated LVPA adjustment will better align payments with costs by recognizing that ESRD facilities furnishing between 4,000 and 8,000 treatments annually generally incur higher costs than higher-volume facilities. Payment Adjustments for Pediatric Patients CMS is finalizing its proposal to modify the payment adjustments for renal dialysis services furnished to pediatric ESRD patients to align with the end of the temporary pediatric payment adjustment in 2027. We are finalizing two permanent changes that will appropriately recognize the higher costs for ESRD facilities that treat pediatric patients. First, we are updating the case-mix adjusters for pediatric patients based on more recent cost data. This addresses the inherent higher costs for patients under 18 years of age. Second, we are finalizing our proposal to begin applying the LVPA to treatments furnished to pediatric ESRD beneficiaries by ESRD facilities that meet the low-volume criteria. Data analysis shows that smaller facilities incur high costs even in the pediatric ESRD patient population. These two changes will result in roughly the same expected spending on pediatric patients, but with payments better targeted toward lower-volume ESRD facilities with higher anticipated resource use and younger pediatric ESRD patients. We are finalizing these changes on a budget-neutral basis. Home and Self-Dialysis Training Add-on Payment Adjustment CMS is finalizing an increase to the payment adjustment amount for home and self-dialysis training from $95.60 to $138.00 beginning in CY 2027. We are also finalizing our proposal to allow the add-on payment adjustment for home and self-dialysis training during the onset period (the first four months of ESRD dialysis), during which the ESRD PPS does not currently pay the home and self-dialysis training add-on adjustment. We are finalizing a reduction to the onset adjustment factor to account for payment overlap during the onset period. The CY 2027 onset adjustment factor is 1.311. These changes will better align the training payment with the actual cost of training patients and caretakers to perform home and self-dialysis. We are finalizing these changes on a budget-neutral basis. Changes to the TDAPA and Post-TDAPA Add-on Payment Adjustment Policies CMS is finalizing several changes to better align payment adjustment amounts for the TDAPA and post-TDAPA add-on payment adjustment with the actual costs of drugs. CMS is finalizing its proposal to use older ASP data when current ASP data are unavailable, such as when the reported ASP reflects zero or negative sales. However, when a drug manufacturer fails to report ASP data to CMS, payment under the TDAPA or Post-TDAPA Add-on Payment Adjustment would be discontinued in accordance with our longstanding conditional ASP policy. CMS is also finalizing a proposal to update the post-TDAPA add-on payment adjustment quarterly through change requests throughout the year, ensuring that the post-TDAPA add-on payment adjustment is based on the most recent pricing and utilization data available. Changes to the Payment for Renal Dialysis Services Furnished to Individuals with AKI As required by section 1834(r) of the Social Security Act, CMS is updating the AKI dialysis payment rate for CY 2027 to $297.52, which is equal to the final CY 2027 ESRD PPS base rate. CMS uses the CY 2027 ESRD PPS wage index to calculate AKI dialysis payments. The increase to the home and self-dialysis training payment adjustment amount under the ESRD PPS will apply to training payments for AKI dialysis. Starting in CY 2027, ESRD facilities are responsible for providing medically necessary phosphate binders to beneficiaries with AKI that are under their direct care. Final Updates to the ESRD QIP The ESRD QIP is authorized by law, and under the program, CMS assesses the total performance of each facility on quality measures specified for a payment year, applies an appropriate payment reduction to each facility that does not meet a minimum total performance score (mTPS), and publicly reports the results. CMS is removing the Hypercalcemia reporting measure and replacing it with the Hyperphosphatemia clinical measure beginning with PY 2029. The Hyperphosphatemia clinical measure will help drive decreases in cardiovascular complications, hospitalizations, and overall mortality by incentivizing additional interventions such as nutritional counseling, phosphorus binding medications, or adjustment of dialysis prescription. Compared to the Hypercalcemia rep

### [Efficacy and cost effectiveness of high volume injections with and without corticosteroid compared with sham for midportion Achilles tendinopathy (Inject trial): randomised controlled trial.](https://doi.org/10.1136/bmj-2026-100807)

- Source: `bmj_abstracts` · published `2026-10-28` · freshness `stale`
- Reading value: `pending` · status: `ineligible`
- Event: `seen` · identity: `pmid:42951276`

<h4>Objective</h4>To test the efficacy and cost effectiveness of high volume injections with and without corticosteroid versus sham injection for midportion Achilles tendinopathy.<h4>Design</h4>Single centred, participant and assessor masked, three arm randomised controlled trial (Inject trial).<h4>Setting</h4>Community.<h4>Participants</h4>From 5 December 2019 and 24 July 2022, 192 adults (18 years or older) diagnosed with midportion Achilles tendinopathy were randomly assigned (1:1:1) to sham (n=64), high volume injection without corticosteroid (n=63), or high volume injection with corticosteroid (n=65).<h4>Interventions</h4>Participants were allocated to sham injection (2-3 mL 0.5% bupivacaine hydrochloride), high volume injection with corticosteroid (50 mL saline and 0.5% bupivacaine with 25 mg hydrocortisone in the first 10 mL), or high volume injection with no corticosteroid (same solution but without hydrocortisone). They received the same exercise programme, education, and activity modification advice.<h4>Main outcome measures</h4>The primary clinical outcome was pain and disability using the validated VISA-A and the primary endpoint was at 52 weeks. The economic outcome was cost per quality-adjusted life-years from a societal perspective.<h4>Results</h4>VISA-A data were obtained from 181 (94%) of 192 participants at 12 months. No clear differences were noted between sham and high volume injection either with (-3.5 (98.33% confidence interval -12.0 to 5.0)) or without corticosteroid (4.0 (-4.2 to 12.3)), and between the two injections (-7.5 (-15.3 to 0.3)), at 12 months. The point estimate was closest to the target difference for the comparison between injections and favoured the arm without steroid. The only differences observed on VISA-A at intermediate time points were a benefit for high volume with corticosteroid versus sham at six weeks (9.2 (1.5 to 16.9)) but worse outcomes for high volume injection with corticosteroid versus high volume injection with no corticosteroid at 26 weeks (-8.6 (-16.4 to -0.7)). The health economic analysis found high volume injection with corticosteroid to be less costly than the sham with similar effectiveness based on quality-adjusted life-years (>0.90 probability of being cost effective at $AUD50 000 and $100 000 willingness-to-pay thresholds). No serious trial related adverse events were recorded.<h4>Conclusion</h4>High volume injections with or without corticosteroids did not improve pain or disability compared with sham at 12 months. Although high volume injections with corticosteroid showed short term clinical benefit and potential reduction in healthcare costs over a year, they were associated with worse longer term clinical outcomes. The clinical data do not support routine use for high volume injections with corticosteroid for midportion Achilles tendinopathy.<h4>Trial registration</h4>Australian New Zealand Clinical Trials Registry ACTRN12619001455156.

### [Harnessing AI for Hypothyroidism Monitoring Using Wearable-Derived Heart Rate Data: Retrospective Cohort Study](https://ai.jmir.org/2026/1/e86030/)

- Source: `jmir_ai` · published `2026-10-28` · freshness `stale`
- Reading value: `pending` · status: `ineligible`
- Event: `seen` · identity: `url:https://ai.jmir.org/2026/1/e86030/`

Background: Hypothyroidism is a prevalent endocrine disorder characterized by an insidious onset and nonspecific symptoms, often leading to significant diagnostic delays. While biochemical thyroid function tests (TFTs) are the gold standard, their invasive nature and cost make them impractical for continuous, proactive screening of at-risk populations. Thyroid hormones exert direct chronotropic effects on the cardiovascular system, and their levels are closely correlated with heart rate (HR). Wearable devices capable of continuous HR monitoring offer a novel opportunity for noninvasive, digital phenotype–based detection. Objective: This study aimed to develop and validate an AI-assisted system that uses wearable-derived sleep HR data as a digital monitoring tool for individuals at risk of hypothyroidism, helping identify those who warrant further confirmatory thyroid testing. Methods: This retrospective cohort study used 1677 paired observations consisting of sleep HR data and serum TFT results, including free thyroxine (T4) and thyroid-stimulating hormone levels, from 413 participants (mean age 43.1, SD 9.7 y; women: n=314, 76%). Participants wore consumer-grade wearables (Fitbit) that recorded HR and sleep data. Each TFT result was paired with sleep HR data extracted from the 15 consecutive nights preceding the blood test. An AI system was developed using a gradient boosting algorithm (LightGBM) to classify thyroid status by integrating baseline TFT results with statistical and temporal HR features. The model’s performance was evaluated using patient-level leave-one-out cross-validation. To examine the benefit of longitudinal data, performance was compared using 1, 2, or 3 prior paired observations as reference points. Results: To ensure clinical relevance and avoid performance inflation from hyperthyroid cases, we prioritized the analysis of a subset containing only euthyroid and hypothyroid data (n=744 paired observations). In this clinically challenging subset, the system achieved an accuracy of 74.6%, with a sensitivity of 82% and a specificity of 73.5% using a single-reference data pair. Incorporating longitudinal HR patterns further enhanced performance; using 3 reference pairs improved accuracy to 78.1% (sensitivity 81.8% and specificity 77.7%). In the overall dataset (n=1677 paired observations), the system demonstrated an accuracy of 80.8%, with a sensitivity of 82% and a specificity of 80.8% using a single-reference pair. Notably, the system achieved high clinical safety by detecting 96.3% of severe overt hypothyroidism cases (free T4 &lt;0.5 ng/dL). Conclusions: This AI-assisted system demonstrates the feasibility of a sensitive, noninvasive longitudinal monitoring tool for hypothyroidism, robustly identifying a high proportion of hypothyroid cases—particularly severe ones—to prioritize at-risk individuals for confirmatory blood tests. Given the predominantly female iatrogenic-withdrawal cohort, prospective sex-balanced validation in primary hypothyroidism is warranted. <img src="https://jmir-production.s3.us-east-2.amazonaws.com/thumbs/a987102a2705d0b3e60602b091360ef2" />

### [Impact of a large measles outbreak associated with transmission in the Americas and Canada's loss of measles elimination status: descriptive cohort study.](https://doi.org/10.1136/bmj-2026-101123)

- Source: `bmj_abstracts` · published `2026-10-28` · freshness `stale`

<h4>Objective</h4>To describe the epidemiological characteristics, public health response, and evidence of international spread of a large measles outbreak that contributed to the loss of Canada's measles elimination status.<h4>Design</h4>Descriptive analysis of the cohort of measles outbreak cases reported to Ontario's public health surveillance system during the outbreak period: epidemiological week of 28 October 2024 to 6 October 2025.<h4>Setting</h4>Ontario, Canada.<h4>Participants</h4>Individuals meeting the outbreak case definition for confirmed or probable measles infection during the outbreak period.<h4>Exposure</h4>Exposure to measles virus associated with the Ontario outbreak and related transmission chains.<h4>Main outcome measures</h4>Demographic characteristics, vaccination status, admission to hospital, complications, pregnancy associated outcomes, mortality, and evidence of international spread based on measles virus genotyping and sequencing. Cases were identified using an outbreak-specific case definition incorporating epidemiological, clinical, laboratory, and viral sequencing criteria. Phylogenetic analysis of measles virus sequences was used to assess evidence of onwards spread to other countries.<h4>Results</h4>The outbreak consisted of 2377 cases (2064 confirmed and 313 probable) over 49 weeks and contributed to the loss of Canada's measles elimination status. Most cases (2146/2377, 90.3%) occurred among members of historically under-vaccinated communities, with limited spillover into the broader population. Among the 2377 cases, approximately three quarters of cases (n=1736, 73.0%) occurred in individuals aged 19 years or younger, 2121 (89.2%) were unvaccinated, and 97 (4.1%) had unknown vaccination status. The outbreak was associated with 166 (7%) admissions to hospital, 13 (<1%) admissions to intensive care unit, 51 (2.1%) infections during pregnancy, 10 (<1%) congenital measles infections, one (<1%) case of encephalitis, and one (<1%) infant death. Sequencing identified a novel N450 distinct sequence identifier that was subsequently detected in countries including the United States of America, Mexico, and the Netherlands. The outbreak strain was designated a World Health Organization named strain (MVs/Ontario.CAN/47.24).<h4>Conclusions</h4>These findings highlight the risk of measles re-establishment in countries that have attained elimination and underscore the importance of culturally appropriate community engagement to build trust in immunisation and prevent future outbreaks. This outbreak shows the fragility of measles elimination in the presence of long standing immunity gaps within interconnected communities across the Americas and the population-level protection that limited broader community transmission.

### [Left Bundle-Branch Area Pacing vs Right Ventricular Pacing for Atrioventricular Block and Preserved Left Ventricular Function: The LEAP-Block Randomized Clinical Trial.](https://doi.org/10.1001/jama.2026.18146)

<h4>Importance</h4>For patients with atrioventricular block requiring high-burden ventricular pacing, the optimal pacing strategy to reduce heart failure (HF) and all-cause mortality remains uncertain. Robust comparative evidence on the clinical outcomes of left bundle-branch area pacing (LBBAP) vs right ventricular pacing (RVP) in this population is needed.<h4>Objective</h4>To compare 24-month clinical outcomes of LBBAP vs RVP in patients with atrioventricular block and preserved left ventricular ejection fraction (LVEF; ≥50%).<h4>Design, setting, and participants</h4>The LEAP-Block study was a prospective, multicenter, randomized, controlled, superiority trial conducted across 14 Chinese hospitals from January 2021 to August 2025, with last follow-up on June 3, 2025. Of 551 patients with LVEF 50% or greater requiring ventricular pacing who underwent eligibility assessment, 499 with anticipated ventricular pacing greater than 40% were randomized.<h4>Interventions</h4>Patients were randomized in a 1:1 ratio to LBBAP (n = 249) or RVP (n = 250).<h4>Main outcomes and measures</h4>The primary end point was a composite of all-cause death, HF events, or upgrade to biventricular pacing at 24 months. Secondary end points included pacing-induced cardiomyopathy and device-related complications.<h4>Results</h4>The primary analysis included 482 patients (LBBAP, n = 242; RVP, n = 240; mean age, 67 years; 46% female). Acute procedure success was comparable between the 2 groups (LBBAP, 237/242 [97.9%] vs RVP, 239/240 [99.6%]; P = .22), with left bundle-branch capture confirmed in 188 of 236 patients (79.7%). LBBAP significantly reduced the primary end point vs RVP (2.4% vs 12.5%, respectively; absolute risk difference, -10.1% [95% CI, -16.8% to -3.4%]; P = .003). Among secondary outcomes, the LBBAP group had fewer HF events (3.4% vs 11.3%; -7.9% [95% CI, -13.0% to -2.8%]). No significant differences were observed in all-cause death, biventricular pacing upgrade, device-related complications, or pacing-induced cardiomyopathy. The 24-month Kaplan-Meier estimated cumulative incidence of the primary end point was 5.0% for LBBAP vs 13.2% for RVP (hazard ratio, 0.37 [95% CI, 0.19 to 0.73]; P = .004). LBBAP superiority was consistent across all prespecified subgroups.<h4>Conclusions and relevance</h4>Among patients with atrioventricular block and preserved LVEF requiring high-burden ventricular pacing, LBBAP was superior to RVP in reducing the risk of the composite end point of all-cause death, HF events, or upgrade to biventricular pacing at 24 months.<h4>Trial registration</h4>ClinicalTrials.gov Identifier: NCT04730921.
